You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. ## assistant

Compared actual conditional/refresher code at common base `64792cc5`, Claude `54c7fecb`, and Codex `639190ac`. **Use the verified Codex head as the foundation, then selectively incorporate Claude’s improvements. Neither side should overwrite the other wholesale.**

Eight concrete differences:

1. **Claude preserves earlier work better when content changes.** Worksheet migration compares graded inputs rather than explanation wording, archives full older attempts, and staged drills retain unaffected questions individually. Codex more often resets an entire changed set.
2. **Claude improves worksheet symbol presentation.** Both sides already have the symbol-naming step. Claude additionally displays semantic condition meanings beside exact highlighted text and explains NOT as denying the complete condition.
3. **Claude better delays model answers.** Its worksheet feedback hides diagrams until the learner has written readbacks and postpones explanations when later objective steps remain.
4. **Codex protects completion more reliably.** Worksheet completion requires explicit final self-review; Claude counts the preceding stages alone. Mixed restore in Codex verifies every question is correct; Claude trusts saved `finished: true`.
5. **Codex retains separate remediation for separate mistakes.** If the student misses both rule equivalence and obligation, Codex provides both fresh checks. Claude substitutes the obligation check and can omit the rule comparison.
6. **Codex saves more lesson interactions.** Its shared `useLessonInteraction` and merged sibling state preserve checks, demonstrations, and exploration choices. Claude’s competing implementations use local `useState`, which loses those choices on reload.
7. **Codex follows the requested help policy better in foundation practice.** It offers Show me after two misses and records assistance honestly. Claude removes that help, though its confirmed restart and preservation of whole previous runs are useful additions.
8. **Claude adds some reasonable teaching/UI refinements.** Concrete word-based diagrams, staged choice ordering, and earlier worked sentence-order examples merit keeping. Indicator tabs versus Codex’s continuous page are a preference conflict, not evidence of a better implementation.

The strongest evidence favors **Codex for functional reliability and verification**, and **Claude for selected instructional refinements and preservation of historical answers**. Existing Codex test/browser evidence applies to its committed head; it does **not** certify a conflict-resolved combination. The combined version needs fresh verification.

## assistant

Recommended root-owned combinations:

- **courseProgress:** Keep Codex readiness, lesson provider, persistent interaction hook, sibling-state merging, and scope reset. Add Claude storage-scope context and accessor.
- **OpeningCourse:** Keep readiness gate and keyed course instance. Pass the storage scope into the combined provider.
- **ConditionalOpeningLesson:** Keep persistent hooks; incorporate Claude fun-run copy and improved wording. Give GateExplorer a new `gate-explorer:fun-run` key because saved `win/bicycle` values do not fit `signup/shirt`.
- **ConditionalReferenceSequence:** Keep persistent hooks and flashcard links. Adopt Claude’s separate original-rule and contrapositive explanations, and kiosk example. Integrated exercise keys already include the sentence, so kiosk state cannot inherit old room answers.
- **DrillDemo:** Adopt Claude initially unopened walkthrough, manual next controls, and replay counter. Retain Codex `frame.spans`/`data-span` support to distinguish adjoining highlights. Remove obsolete `STEP_MS`.
- **Refresher:** Keep Codex hints, two-miss Show me, assistance, and answer/explanation/Next gates. Adopt Claude’s guard against moving focus on a restored answer. Consolidate draft/attempt APIs with compatibility aliases, latest callback refs, and skipped initial mount write; coordinate canonical names with the mixed-drill worker.
- **Refresher test:** Keep the default wrong-answer hint assertion and the assertion that no model diagram appears.
- **OpeningCourseContent test:** Conflict is equivalent regex versus exact assertion. Either works. Preserve continuous-indicator expectations and adjust changed examples without weakening behavior coverage.

A wholesale Claude resolution would lose persistence and help/completion safeguards. A wholesale Codex resolution would miss useful historical-work migration, clearer feedback timing, and improved walkthrough controls.

## assistant

One actionable source finding, confidence 85%:

**Worksheet migration preserves passed condition stages after the grading key becomes stricter.** `sameGraded` compares only the sentence and indicators, but `checkConditionSpans` also depends on the separately changed span key. An earlier accepted highlight such as “do addition” can remain passed after the corrected key requires “can do addition,” including its final review.

On restore, revalidate previously passed condition spans against the current key. Archive the prior attempt, invalidate conditions and dependent later stages when it fails, and preserve earlier indicator work and drafts. Add a saved-state regression for a missing modal.

No other ≥80% actionable issue found in the inspected merged course/lesson/refresher saving callbacks. This is source review, not browser acceptance; the drills worker was still finishing during inspection.
